decisions: **B271** the report computes every E13 figure over the EXECUTED rows with the walked-day index passed explicitly, streaming the other 306,967 rows past and counting them -- `pf.metrics` derives everything from the trades plus that index, so the two are identical, and 495,312 resident dataclasses to say so is a cost with no answer attached. **B272** the per-year table is one `pf.metrics` call per calendar year seeded at that year's OWN opening equity, so a year's drawdown is a fall inside that year; the reviewed pure module was NOT changed for a report's convenience. **B274** the per-symbol table carries no 15-minute path and prints `pf.INTRADAY_PATH_NOT_SUPPLIED` to say so; the path is a portfolio statistic and 204 of them would multiply the observations by 204 for a figure E13 never asks for per symbol. **B275** the buy&hold benchmark is computed in BOTH price domains and NEITHER is published (Q-23) -- the spec fixes the construction and is silent on the domain, and over ten years the silence decides the number. **B276** refusals are priced ONLY where a counterfactual exists (the crashed run's retained shards); The filename IS a date and CLAUDE.md's code standards forbid a hardcoded date; the loader validates it as a bare FILENAME (no separator, no `..`, not absolute) so the pin can never resolve outside the cache the operator snapshots. No default and no "newest wins" fallback -- that fallback is precisely what the ruling retired.
  - B235 `backtest.latest_cached_master` is DELETED rather than deprecated, and newest-by-filename selection moves to `instrument_master.newest_cached_master`, beside `cached_masters`, where it is cache inspection for the operator CLI and the migration evidence. A selector that still exists on the run path is not retired, only unused today.
  - B236 `trade_evidence` (chunk-8's sweep) is pinned too, though it is NOT the run path. It computes POCs, and the repo should have ONE tick source rather than two that agree until the day they do not. Its own docstring's premise -- "the tick sizes it reads are per-symbol constants, not a daily quantity" -- is what Q-20 measured false.
  - B237 The pin rides on `RunSpec` as `master_file` + `master_sha256`, inside the spec digest, rather than as a manifest-only field. That is what makes the resume refusal free: `_resume_state` already compares the digest, so the ruling's "a resume under any other master REFUSES" needed no new branch. Both halves are carried because a filename alone is not enough -- a vendor file can be overwritten in place.
  - B238 `build_runner` REFUSES a `master_path` whose name is not the configured pin. An explicit path is the preflight CONFIRMING what it resolved and printed to the operator, never an override; without the guard, threading the path would have re-opened by argument the door `config.yaml` closed.
  - B239 The manifest gains `caveat_basis` -- the register entries the CAVEAT quotes -- kept separate from `per_symbol`, which is the universe the run WALKED. Without it the "caveat quotes its own register" invariant could never pass on any run smaller than the full universe, because IOC and TATASTEEL are not in a five-symbol pilot. CONTEXT 4.6 (v1.5) requires the caveat's figures be quoted from the register's current figures; this makes that checkable from the manifest alone.
  - B240 The calibration pack classifies a mismatch as MOVED (the pin changed a figure -> STOP) or PUBLISHED-SLIP (a published figure disagrees with arithmetic the pin does not touch), decided by RECOMPUTING the day under the OTHER cached snapshot. Collapsing the two would either hide a real tick move inside a known typo or halt a run over one.
  - B241 The throughput projection has THREE terms, not one, and its samples are spread EVENLY across the universe. A blended smoke figure x102 would have projected both wiring terms at 1/102 of their cost; and an alphabetically-first sample gave 10.4 s/symbol at n=2 against 13.4 at n=10, which reads as superlinearity and is really the early alphabet carrying less history. The session scan is projected by MONTH-FILE count (20,460 vs 1,261 sampled), which is the work it actually does.
  - B242 Five committed evidence/recovery generators still called `config.path("data_dir")`, a key RESUME-1's rename retired; each would have raised on its first store access, including the `chunk9b_out_of_session.py` this session was required to run. Repaired in place, one word each; no output value can move and none was regenerated -- their measurements belong to their own sessions.
  - B243 The card's chunk-6 oracle citation (BHARTIARTL 2026-07-24, POC 1913.9, 25 rows) disagrees with this repo's record (2026-07-17; 1913.9/25 rows are the TRADER's chart reading, not an engine output). BOTH dates were computed and both reported; neither was quietly preferred. 2026-07-17 reproduces the engine's 1914.60 in 26 rows exactly.

decisions:

  - B194 On the 15-minute path the Rs 100 round-trip cost is charged at the ENTRY mark, not at the exit. The round trip is committed the moment the position opens, and charging it there makes each trade's LAST mark equal its realized net PnL exactly -- which is what lets each day's last observation equal that day's closing equity instead of stepping by Rs 100 per trade at the exits. Asserted as `path_reconciles` and as a pack invariant.
  - B195 The quartile estimator is the one thing Q-16(a) does not fix. Chosen: linear interpolation between order statistics (R's and numpy's default, type 7), computed exactly in Fractions -- because it is what a reviewer reproduces with `numpy.percentile(x, [25, 75])`, and both hand-computed fixtures were verified against it. The estimator is named IN the printed definition, so the architect can overrule it by reading the pack.

decisions:
  - B178 The run ledger is per-symbol SHARDS plus a `progress.json`, and the single `ledger.jsonl` is published only when every symbol is complete. A shard is written (atomic_io, fsync, atomic replace) only once its symbol is fully walked, so an interruption INSIDE a symbol leaves no shard for it and the resume re-walks exactly what it lost -- duplicate-freedom by construction rather than by de-duplication. Proved both ways in tests and in the pack.
  - B179 Nothing in a ledger row or a manifest is read from a clock; the only timestamps are CANDLE stamps. That is what makes a completed re-run byte-identical. The two commit-dependent manifest fields (`code_sha`, `config_digest`) stay ON DISK, where pinning a run to its commit is the point, but are excluded from `stable_manifest_digest`, which is what the evidence pack quotes -- otherwise the pack could not regenerate byte-identically after the next commit.
  - B180 `RunSpec.digest()` COVERS the code SHA, so resuming a run whose code has moved is REFUSED rather than silently mixed. A ledger is a function of one spec and one code state; the inconvenience is the point.
  - B181 CONTEXT 7-E2's own detection ("candle data ... outside 09:15-15:30") is executed literally: a date whose stored candles all fall outside the session is a NON-STANDARD session, is refused under its own reason, and is removed from the trading calendar BEFORE any bias pair is built -- so it is not the previous trading day of anything, exactly as the Q-5 weekend ruling treats a Saturday session. The detection is market-wide (a session is a property of the market, not of a symbol). NOTE FOR 9B: the scan currently runs at wiring time over the run's universe and span, which on a full-history run is one extra pass over the minute store; 9B should cache its result beside the ledger.
  - B182 `capital_reference` is a rupee amount and `margin_basis` is a MULTIPLE of it (CONTEXT 3.5's own example: 1,00,000 cash vs a 5,00,000 typical-MIS tier). Both keys are OPTIONAL and both are NULL, so no flag is computed and every output prints the pending sentence verbatim. The shape question rides with Q43 in QUESTIONS.md, non-blocking, because no flag value depends on it until the answer arrives.
  - B183 MFE/MAE per trade (CONTEXT 7-E13 names them and fixes no convention) are measured over the candles the position was actually HELD -- after the entry candle, up to and including the exit candle, which is exactly CONTEXT 3.4-5's monitoring window under E7 -- and are signed so MFE >= 0 and MAE <= 0. They are computed in the RUNNER (the layer that holds the bars) and stored in the ledger, which keeps `portfolio.py` pure over rows.
  - B184 The daily equity series covers every WALKED trading day, not only the days that traded: a flat day is a real observation for a daily Sharpe, and dropping it would annualize a different sample. E13's Long / Short columns and the per-symbol table are computed over the SAME day index for the same reason.
  - B185 The running extreme for drawdown and run-up starts at the run's OPENING capital, not at the first day's close. A fall measured from a close that came AFTER the low is not a fall anyone lived through; a `peak_day` / `trough_day` of `None` means "the opening capital", counted as the observation before the first day.
  - B186 Sharpe uses the SAMPLE standard deviation (n-1); Sortino's downside deviation is measured against E13's own zero target and averaged over ALL observations. Both return `None` -- never 0 -- when they are undefined (fewer than two returns, or a perfectly flat sample): an undefined ratio reported as zero is a lie a report would print in bold.
  - B187 CAGR is computed over the WALKED calendar span with a 365-day year, and is `None` when the final equity is at or below zero (a negative base has no real root).
  - B188 The buy and hold benchmark buys exact FRACTIONAL units -- rounding to whole shares would make an "equal-weight" benchmark depend on each stock's price level -- and a symbol with no close on the first trade date is EXCLUDED and NAMED in the note rather than back-filled.
  - B189 The "%" beside a largest win or loss is that trade's return on its OWN notional (qty x entry), the only percentage a single trade has; the "% of gross" figure beside it is its share of gross profit or gross loss.
  - B190 A bias that cannot be assembled refuses ONE DAY, not the symbol: the runner finds the first day of the span whose CONTEXT 3.2 pair the calendar can build and seeds the carry there, and every earlier day is refused under its own counted reason. Letting the engine's exception through would lose a whole symbol's history to its own first morning.
  - B191 The pack's universe-wide materiality scan compares two ENGINE answers (the orchestration run with the real table and with an empty one, each seeded 40 calendar days back) rather than two isolated pairs. Comparing isolated pairs counted "this pair fires no rule" as a change and inflated the result from 47 to 53; the run-level comparison is the one that means something.
  - B192 Rare-shape counts and the outcome partition are DERIVED from the ledger rows at manifest time rather than accumulated during the walk. A resumed run replays shards it did not walk, so a running counter would undercount and the manifest would stop being byte-identical -- and a derived count is one a reader can recompute from the committed ledger alone.
questions: **NEW Q-16 (class A, OPEN -- STOP, blocks exactly two CONTEXT 7-E13 metric entries).** (a) E13 lists "outliers" and fixes no definition at all -- no threshold, no rule, no reference -- so the metric is NOT COMPUTED and the pack prints the reason instead of a number (a test asserts no outlier count is ever printed). (b) E13's intra-trade / intrabar drawdown and run-up need an intraday equity PATH, and a take-all portfolio of concurrent intraday trades has none the ledger can observe (it holds each trade's MFE and MAE but not WHEN inside the day each occurred; five trades ran concurrently even in this pilot). It IS computed, under one explicitly stated worst-case construction, and LABELLED PROVISIONAL everywhere it appears -- on the pilot it reads Rs 18,844.65 against the close-to-close Rs 12,761.75, a 48% difference on the number a trader judges risk by, which is why it is the architect's to rule. tests: 1306 passed / 0 failed offline (1253 inherited + 53 new). Fixtures touched: NONE.
decisions:
  - B141 GATE 1P is a CONTAINMENT, not an equality. The ruling's own words are "must sit INSIDE", and the asymmetry is real: the exchange's daily high is the maximum over EVERY trade, including auction and block prints the continuous 1-minute series never carries, so a fold high BELOW the daily high is ordinary microstructure while one ABOVE it is impossible on raw prices. The other reading (the two-sided equality auction relief's condition (b) uses) would fail every day carrying an auction print. A wrong SCALE moves both ends the same way, so one of them always escapes -- which is why containment still catches 0.1x and 5x.
  - B142 gate 1P's denominator is EVERY stored day, and a day with no raw daily row FAILS it. The ruling says so in as many words, and it is what closes REVIEW_5B finding Q4: those 178 days were in neither numerator nor denominator, so they were neither passed nor counted as excluded. Every stored symbol-day now sits in exactly one bucket.
  - B143 the recovery pass measures its floors from the STORE, spending no credentialed call. The observable a probe buys is `fetched/raw`; the ingest wrote `stored = fetched / k_applied`, so `event-in` is "the stored day is contained in raw" (gate 1P itself) and `event-out` is "the stored day multiplied BACK by the event's own factor is contained". Identical oracle, identical tolerance, identical bisection -- and the ruling's own closing note is that both sides of the check are LOCAL. It also makes the measurement reproducible offline by anyone holding the two stores, which a live probe never is.
  - B144 the 80% quarantine floor is recorded as a Class-B choice of this repo's (finding C16); the old comment attributed it to "the architect" with no ruling or card behind it. Nothing downstream depends on the exact number -- a quarantined symbol's days are excluded and counted either way.
  - B145 `_CLUSTER_RATE` (0.90), `_SCATTER_RATE` (0.50) and the un-asked-for `PATTERN_MIXED` bucket are recorded as Class-B (finding C16). The Q-12-addendum ruling names two patterns and no numbers; these are this repo's reading of its own words, they are REPORT-ONLY, and nothing in the run consumes the verdict.
  - B146 the gate-1P cluster signature is measured as a STEP, not as a whole-span failure rate: the longest old-end block of failing days that ENDS on a failing day, at least MIN_CLIFF_DAYS long, failing at >= FLOOR_HUNT_ERA_CLIFF_RATE, with a clean remainder above it. Same two constants as the FIX-4 volume cliff, and it degenerates to exactly that cliff when the whole span fails. Measured why: NMDC's clustered era fails 135 of 244 days -- 55% on a whole-span reading and a perfect step at 133 -- so a rate test would have admitted nothing.
  - B147 a cluster is a property of an ERA and a floor belongs to an EVENT, so the admitted candidate list is the clustered era's OWN committed chain (its events with a factor not already 1). The bisection then decides which of them actually carries a splice -- NMDC admitted two and the search answered "no splice" for the wrong one. An UN-PROVABLE era admits nothing at all: it commits no chain, so there is no factor to drop and no floor could change one stored price.
  - B148 acceptance is a DRY RUN first and greedy per event: each floor is scored by rescaling the stored days arithmetically (`stored x k_before/k_after` on the price side, the inverse on the volume side) and re-running both per-day gates. A floor is kept only if MORE days pass BOTH gates and no fewer pass gate 1, so a floor that improves only ONE gate is refused -- BSE's volume floor at 2017-12-20 would have moved 80 days into gate 1 while none of them became usable, and it was discarded rather than committed. A rejected floor is never written, so the store is never touched speculatively.
  - B149 QUARANTINE still reads the gate-1 rate alone. The ruling makes a gate-1P failure exclude a DAY and says nothing about symbols; changing the quarantine trigger would be a silent decision of a different kind. The gate-1P rate IS added to the floor-hunt scope, because that is where the ruling's own hunt lives -- SRF sits at 99.5% on gate 1 and stored 216 days at five times the traded price.
  - B150 (finding Q5) a ledger row's floor CLAIMS are re-derived from the MAP that carries their evidence, on every re-gate, never from the pass's own result. `run_floor_pass` ASSIGNED, so a floor measured earlier and carried forward was erased -- ASTRAL and NESTLEIND each held a resolved floor in force while their rows read `floors_resolved = 0` and the report printed "no event carries a vendor application floor". Reading the claims off the map makes the claim/evidence invariant true by construction and preserves `floor_ex_dates`, decision B138's forced-override handle.
  - B151 committing a fresh floor MERGES with the floors the map already carried. `with_floors` replaces the tuple, so the Q-14 pass would otherwise have dropped the FIX-3/FIX-4 floors of any symbol it recovered -- the same class of loss as Q5, one level down.
  - B152 (the recording REVIEW_5B asked for; B127's widening). B127's recorded text says a floor is "carried only when the event still resolves to the same committed price factor". FIX-4 widened `carry_floors_forward` with two further KEEP branches -- a floor is also kept when the fresh map commits NOTHING for the event (`after is None`) or commits exactly ABSENT. The widening is correct and necessary: a floored event SHOULD resolve to absent below its floor, so a floor that forces an event out of every era makes its canonical factor 1, which the same-factor test reads as a change and drops, re-opening a hunt that would measure the same floor again forever. In both kept cases dropping the event from a day's chain is the identity, so the carry cannot alter one stored price. Recorded here because the superseded B127 text was the only carry-rule statement the architect scanned.
  - B153 the containment tolerance is defined ONCE, in `quality_gates` (`PRICE_CONTAINMENT_MIN_PAISE` / `PRICE_CONTAINMENT_REL` / `price_containment_limit`), and `vendor_adjustment` imports it; likewise the raw-gap-near-zero predicate is now one `same_price_domain` shared by the signature gate and the report classifier (finding C13). The era oracle and the per-day gate must be the same number by construction.
  - OPERATOR ACTION, disclosed: after the run, one derived-index maintenance pass re-derived every ledger row's floor CLAIMS from its committed map using `claim_floors_from_map` -- the same code path the runner now uses on every re-gate (the fix landed after the run had started). Five rows changed, all in the direction finding Q5 named: ASTRAL and NESTLEIND now claim the floors their maps have carried since FIX-4, and DIXON/RELIANCE/VEDL gained the ex-dates their counts already implied. Nothing was measured and no gate was re-run; the map is the authority and this only made the derived index agree with it.

decisions:
  - B132 clause (ii) is read STRICTLY: in an un-provable era only the event being floored may take our own CONTEXT 4.2 factor; every OTHER event must carry a previously COMMITTED source or the era is refused. Measured why: on IOC the vendor applies NO special dividend, so filling an uncommitted slot with `ours` puts BOTH hypotheses ~3% off and turns every decisive probe undecided. Filling it would be the policy-guessing measurement replaced.
  - B133 the gate-3 signature is the NEAREST-HYPOTHESIS test on MAGNITUDES (`|raw gap| < |k-1|/2`), not a percentage threshold: the two models predict gaps of size 0 and |k-1|. It is scale-free (a 5:1 split and a 5% special dividend are judged alike) and admits EXACTLY the eleven rows the architect named, rejecting the six that are a different defect.
  - B134 a floor sitting at or above the ex-date ("absent from every chain in our history") is a resolved outcome, not an abandoned search -- but it requires the newest, the oldest AND a midpoint probe to all answer event-out. One probe is not a measurement of a span; a single event-in or undecided leaves it UNRESOLVED.
  - B135 acceptance is the MAP BUILDER's own oracle: measured floors are passed INTO build_map rather than layered onto a committed map, so a promoted era satisfies the same 2-paise containment and the same unwidened gate-1 band as every other era. A floored event is also no longer an unknown for the probe-gap guard, which is what lets a cascade of "under-determined" older eras unwind (VEDL: 13 eras promoted).
  - B136 the hunt COMPOSES over at most MAX_FLOOR_HUNT_ROUNDS = 3 rounds: each round's promotions become the next round's committed sources. Bounded and deterministic -- a round that resolves no new floor, or promotes no era, is the last; the probe cache is shared so no day is fetched twice.
  - B137 the baseline classifier gains `as-fetched-floored` (the vendor's own chain on a floored day), and EVERY provenance is now corrected by the multiple it matched rather than by the day's target chain. Identical arithmetic on a floorless map; without it a newly-promoted era's days classify UNKNOWN and the floor repairs nothing. Coinciding candidates are de-duplicated so the derived tolerance cannot collapse.
  - B138 a floor a previous pass measured is its OWN admission for a re-measurement (`force_ex_dates` bypasses the signature gate): the repair erases the failure signature that admitted it, so requiring the signature again makes a second measurement impossible -- the trap B125 caught on the failure-rate gate, one level up.
  - B139 the Q-5 ruling reaches the MEASUREMENT days: a weekend-dated session is not a trading day, so it is neither chosen as a probe day nor folded into an era. Enforced at BOTH ends because a probe window is a date RANGE and the vendor returns the excluded session inside it. Measured: NSE's Saturday 2024-05-18 recovers 0.259/0.196 of raw volume on BDL/INOXWIND against 0.500/0.246 on the sessions beside it, and because gate-1 must hold on EVERY probe day it made the only correct chain un-provable -- 2,391 stored days. No oracle was widened: band, containment and candidate sets are byte-identical.
  - B140 the run-level quarantine ceiling is scoped to the run's own symbols INSIDE the loop as well as before it (B131 fixed only the pre-flight), so a `--symbols` subset is judged on its own outcomes. Identical behaviour for a full-universe run.
  - OPERATOR REPAIR 1, disclosed: two runner processes briefly overlapped on the same store (the first survived a wrapper exit) and a parquet write collided. Both write atomically and the map application is idempotent by baseline classification, so nothing was torn; but 63 ledger rows were written under that race, so their `rebuild_discipline`/`floor_discipline` markers were CLEARED and every one was re-derived by a single clean process. Clearing a marker schedules recomputation from the stores; it asserts no result.
  - OPERATOR REPAIR 2, disclosed: BDL's map was rebuilt by the aborted sweep before the fold-level Q-5 filter existed and was stamped with the current model marker optimistically. The marker was corrected to the model that actually built it and the row re-opened; the re-measurement then settled BDL at 99.7% on its own evidence.

decisions:
  - B122 a relieved day is handed to gate 2 as `volume_reconciled=True`. The completeness ruling excludes for missing minutes only "ON A DAY WHERE GATE-1 ALSO FAILS"; a relieved day's verdict is pass-by-relief, and relief conditions (b)/(c) are direct evidence nothing was lost -- the exact hypothesis that trigger exists to catch. The report counts relieved-days-also-carrying-tradeless-minutes so the size is visible (92 of 432).
  - B123 floors apply to PROVABLE eras only. An un-provable era has no committed per-event chain to drop an event FROM; the ruling's own closing sentence keeps it un-provable. This is why IOC/OIL/GAIL/PETRONET/BEL/HINDPETRO could not be floored -- their old eras are un-provable (see the residual gate-3 rows).
  - B124 the hunt scope reads the EFFECTIVE gate-1 rate (relief included). A PNBHOUSING-shaped symbol whose above-ceiling failures are a market property has no adjustment problem, and ~11 probes an event would be measuring noise.
  - B125 within a hunted symbol an event is searched only when its pre-ex provable-era span fails >= 10% of its days -- with a FORCED override for an event a previous pass already floored, because a span repaired BY a floor no longer fails and the gate would otherwise hide its own justification. Every skip is recorded with its reason.
  - B126 the stored-day baseline gains `over-divided-twice` (measured: CANBK's pre-2022-05-10 days sat at exactly 25x raw = 1/k^2 for its 0.2 split -- damage from the superseded one-way rebuild) plus the two floor provenances `pre-floor-divided` / `floor-overreached`; hypotheses are generated from the ERA chain (not the floor-reduced chain) and the match tolerance is DERIVED from the candidate set (half the closest relative gap, capped at 2%) so extending the set can never let two hypotheses claim one ratio.
  - B127 a measured floor is CARRIED onto a rebuilt map (a floor is a property of the vendor's archive, not of when we last looked), carried only when the event still resolves to the same committed price factor, and a dropped floor RE-OPENS the hunt. A record may only claim a floor while its map carries the evidence -- enforced at resume level too.
  - B128 a transient SmartApiError leaves the floor hunt OPEN for the next run (CONTEXT 4.3: access-denied bursts are NORMAL and retried, never an answer); deterministic failures close it so a run cannot loop forever. Found live -- CANBK's first re-measure died on a rate-limit burst.
  - B129 "price-suspect" in ruling 3(ii) is read as EVERY ParseException on the symbol -- the same set the Q-11 routing rule already treats as map-forcing. An informational unparsed subject resolves to `absent` (cost 1 < measured's 2) and changes no chain, so the conservative reading costs a probe window and nothing else.
  - B130 a compound node's volume `ours` is the product of the SHARE-COUNT components only (1.0 for a cash dividend, None if any component has no volume rule); the node is share-count only when EVERY component is, so a mixed node never claims volume-scales-with-price.
  - B131 the halt ceiling counts only quarantined symbols WITHIN the run's own symbol list. Previously a `--symbols` subset was unstartable the moment anything anywhere was quarantined -- the operator's own diagnostic path refused for a reason unrelated to the symbol. Identical behaviour for a full-universe run.

Only the EVENT ex-date was seeded, never a floor value -- the search re-measured the floor from the vendor and independently reproduced 2022-05-10 in 13 probes, identical to the original measurement. The ledger is a derived index (RunLedger.load's own docstring); the seed scheduled a re-measurement, it did not assert a result.

decisions:
  - B111 The profile window is a PARAMETER with a spec-cited module constant as its default, NOT a new config.yaml key. Q-8 is OPEN, and a config knob would let any later session flip a strategy-critical window without an architect ruling; the 9-candle alternative is reachable only from the evidence generator and is named `evidence-9-candle` in the code.
  - B112 The window is held as a COUNT of 15-minute candles from the session open and the 1-minute stamps are DERIVED (`09:15 + 15*n - 1`). CONTEXT 3.3 states the same window twice (stamps 09:15..11:14, and "the candle closing at 11:15 is included"); deriving one from the other means the two descriptions cannot drift, and no clock literal is typed into the engine.
  - B113 Row volumes are exact `fractions.Fraction`, not floats. CONTEXT 7-E11 bans float equality and the POC's tie rule IS an equality test -- with Fractions the "maximum row" comparison and the tie are exact, and the profile sums to the window volume EXACTLY rather than to within a tolerance.
  - B114 `totalTicks` rounds half-EVEN (Python's own `round`, and the rounding minute_unadjust already uses for money). On the tick grid the division is exact, so the mode only ever decides an off-grid residual -- but it is stated rather than inherited from a float.
  - B115 The ticks-per-row TIE keeps the SMALLER tpr (the finer profile). This is CONTEXT 3.3's one silence, RAISED as Q-13 with measured impact rather than chosen quietly; the interim is the direction that reproduces all 25 frozen `poc_prorata` values (the other moves six of them).
  - B116 The topmost row's ALLOCATION bound stretches to `top` when the tick grid rounds short of it, while its MIDPOINT stays on the grid edge. That keeps CONTEXT 3.3's "the topmost row includes top" true in both senses -- no traded price falls outside the profile, and the rows tile [bottom, top] so prorata conserves volume exactly in both rounding directions.
  - B117 A point bar (`high == low`) puts its FULL volume in the containing row, per CONTEXT 3.3's own sentence. This DIFFERS from the archived PoC script (`poc/poc3_volume_poc_test.py`), whose prorata branch computes a zero overlap for such a bar and drops its volume. The difference is invisible on the calibration data (measured: 0 point bars in all 25 frozen windows, so F7 is unaffected) but it is required by the conservation property and by the spec's text.
  - B118 An empty window returns no POC under its own reason code (`no-poc-no-candles-in-window`), distinct from the two gate-1 reasons. It is the empty set -- `max(high)` over no bars does not exist -- not a re-introduced minute count; measured at 9-11 days per symbol over ~2,400 stored days, mostly non-standard sessions.

A structural test forbids any store-write symbol (`write_bars`, `record_window`, `atomic_write`, `rebuild_symbol_raw`) appearing in it -- this session's read-only store rule proved, not promised.

The card text predates the amendment; the test names the ruling in its docstring so the difference is not read as drift.

Three pre-existing assertions were UPDATED to the ruled behaviour, not weakened, each with the reason in its docstring: the ACME rights' volume source is now `price-factor` (the ruled candidate order) with a NEW companion test proving a divergence gate 1 cannot absorb still FORCES `measured`; the synthetic-vendor report assertion likewise; and the reroute's un-provable branch now asserts the day is left untouched rather than restored.
decisions:
  - B101 The Q-12 estimator is applied wherever a volume scalar is FORMED, not only in the per-era solve: `_refine_scalars` takes the MINIMUM over the union of an event's price-passing probe days instead of the median. The ruling names one estimator; leaving the cross-era refinement on a median would have re-introduced the bias it forbids at the last step.
  - B102 A chosen price factor of exactly 1 is NOT offered as the volume `price-factor` candidate -- that IS what `absent` means, and relabelling a vendor omission as a measurement would make the audit row lie. A carried price-factor event whose price flipped to absent in an older era emits `absent`, so the volume tracks the price flip honestly.
  - B103 The measured VOLUME candidate is withheld entirely below 3 price-passing probe days (the ruling's "minimum 3 such days, else no measured-volume candidate"), which can newly mark un-provable an era that a 1-2-day median used to fit. That is the ruling: two points are not a measurement of a contaminated observable.
  - B104 Gate 2 with an UNRUN gate 1 (`volume_reconciled=None`, no raw daily row) keeps the pre-amendment behaviour and excludes on missing minutes. The ruling's licence is "gate-1 PASSES"; an unrun gate has not passed.
  - B105 The negative-values trigger tests VOLUME and OPEN as well as high/low/close. CONTEXT 4.5's OHLC-sanity trigger deliberately omits `open`, but "negative values" names no field, and a negative share count is as impossible as a negative price and would poison gate 1's own sum.
  - B106 Applying a map to a TABLE-path store uses the NET factor `k_map / k_table` in ONE division, never a second full division on top of the first (which would divide by `k_table` twice and round twice). A net factor of 1 is the exact identity.
  - B107 The quarantine-recovery route decision is STICKY on the ledger row (`map_required_by_recovery`). The classifier reads the CA table, which does not know a recovery happened, so without this a re-processing run would drop a recovered symbol back onto the price-blind cheap path that quarantined it.
  - B108 The map-backed rebuild CLASSIFIES a stored day's baseline (`stored_day_baseline`: raw / as-fetched / over-divided / unknown) instead of asking the one-way "is it raw?". The one-way test is wrong in the dangerous direction, because "not raw" means "divide it" -- measured: 37 genuinely-raw ASHOKLEY days sit 0.1%-0.5% off the bhavcopy high (a print no continuous 1-minute candle holds), were called not-raw, and were divided twice. Recognising `1/k` as a hypothesis both prevents that and REPAIRS a doubly-divided day, making the rebuild idempotent by construction rather than by bookkeeping. An unidentifiable baseline is LEFT UNTOUCHED and counted -- never guessed. `_stored_day_is_raw` is kept for the Q-10 factor-table path, where the alternative to "raw" is "leave it alone".

decisions:
  - B86 Per-event measurement, not policy (Q-11). `R(D)=fetched/raw` (fold HIGH/LOW vs the RAW daily store) is the direct observable = product of the vendor's applied factors in (D,F]. Days sharing an in-window event set form an ERA; the map resolves each event to {ours, measured k̂, not-applied} for price and (independently) volume, arbitrated by 2-paise price containment + gate-1's band. Working BACKWARDS from F; min-cost prefers ours>absent>measured; a source is decided at an event's newest appearance and carried older; ONLY a no-ours event (demerger) may FLIP measured<->absent across eras (the era-inconsistency). At most one freshly-solved unknown per era -> measurement, never free fitting.
  - B87 Single measured scalar per event = the MEDIAN over ALL its pre-ex probe days (phase-2 refinement), isolating the one event by dividing out the others' resolved factors -- exactly the ruling's "median ratio over pre-ex probe days". This tightened RELIANCE's 2016 era from 3->2 paise and is why the rights is ONE scalar (price 0.987305 / vol 0.987707) across the 2016 AND 2019 eras.
  - B88 Price provability uses the MEDIAN containment residual (not max): the fetched fold high/low can differ from the official daily high/low by a few paise on an odd day for a microstructure reason (an odd-lot/block trade the bhavcopy counts but the continuous 1-min series does not -- the same reason gate-1's band skews positive). A WRONG factor misses by hundreds of paise on EVERY day, so the median separates choices cleanly; the identity era's lone 10-paise day (2024-11-07) stays provable. The -0.1% gate-1 floor is NOT widened (ruling).
  - B89 Rights & demerger VOLUME are measured too (independently of price). The vendor scaled volume for the rights (measured 0.987707, != our TERP) and for the demerger in its recent era (0.907921) -- the exact reason FIX-3's share-count-only k_shares failed gate-1 at -1.24%/-10.14%. This RESOLVES decision B81 by measurement rather than a convention.
  - B90 Ingest consumes the map (`backfill_symbol(..., adjustment_map=...)` -> `unadjust_with_map`); the Q-10 factor-table path stays as the fallback for clean bonus/split-only symbols (TCS) and when no map exists. `rebuild_symbol_raw_with_map` carries a gate-1 IDENTITY GUARD (skip a day whose stored volume already reconciles -> already RAW), making a rebuild of an already-raw store a NO-OP and safe to re-run (unlike the Q-10 rebuild).
  - B91 Era key = the in-window event set {ex : D < ex <= F}; the map keys eras by it and a day whose era is not in the map (unprobed) or is un-provable returns None from factors_for_day -> gate 1 excludes and counts it (CONTEXT 7-E3, surgical clamp). A symbol whose vendor floor falls WITHIN one era key with no intervening event to split it (a demerger-only symbol) has a bimodal era -> no single factor reconciles -> un-provable + excluded (tested). An EMPTY in-window set (a recent post-last-CA day) is the exact identity (1,1) even without a probed identity era -- a recent RAW day is never wrongly excluded.
  - B92 Adversarial-review hardening (an in-session multi-agent review found real defects; all fixed with regression tests, none changed the RELIANCE/TCS results): (1) `_price_contained` now checks EVERY probe day within max(2 paise, 0.1% of raw) -- NOT the median -- so a BIMODAL era (the vendor floor inside one era key, a minority of days on a different factor that gate-1 volume cannot see) is caught and marked un-provable; the 0.1% relative floor still absorbs market microstructure (fold-vs-daily-high, ~0.01%) while rejecting the smallest wrong factor (~0.33%). (2) build_map RE-VALIDATES the phase-2-refined chain against the oracle before committing provable=True (the refinement can shift a scalar). (3) A PROBE GAP (an era introducing >1 event not covered by a newer era -> per-event attribution under-determined) is marked un-provable, never a fabricated decomposition. (4) `rebuild_symbol_raw_with_map`'s identity guard now checks price AND volume (a price-only special dividend leaves volume reconciled; a volume-only guard would skip it with price still adjusted -- a wrong price to the backtest) and skips a day with no raw daily row. (5) measure_era drops zero-volume/degenerate probe days (no div-by-zero); build_map refuses two events sharing an ex-date (STOP, not silent-collapse).

## [2026-07-25 02:40] incident · fix · done
scope: recovered data/daily_store after a power-loss truncated its ledger mid-backfill (REVIEW_2 F6 manifesting; architect approved pulling the F6 fix forward from chunk 13). Diagnosed read-only, quarantined the corrupt files (never deleted), rebuilt the ledger from the surviving monthlies via a new permanent --rebuild-ledger CLI, hardened atomic_io with fsync, and made a corrupt ledger raise clear guidance instead of a pyarrow traceback. Scope was recovery + the F6 hardening ONLY — no strategy code, no chunk work.
files: edited src/acumen/atomic_io.py (fsync file before os.replace in both writers; best-effort POSIX dir fsync; Windows limitation documented), src/acumen/daily_store.py (DailyStore.rebuild_ledger_from_monthlies(); startup-integrity error in outcomes() naming --rebuild-ledger; REBUILD_LEDGER_COMMAND + REBUILT_LEDGER_REASON constants; import url_for), src/acumen/backfill_daily.py (--rebuild-ledger flag + _rebuild_ledger printer; --from/--to now optional with a clear error; main() maps DailyStoreError to a clean exit 2); created tests/test_incident_recovery.py (12 tests). DATA (gitignored, not committed): quarantined 3 corrupt files to data/daily_store/_quarantine/2026-07-25/ with a MANIFEST; wrote a fresh data/daily_store/ledger.parquet. NO fixture touched; no file under poc/ touched; CONTEXT.md/plan.md untouched.
tests: 686 passed / 0 failed (was 674 at the chunk-3 review; +12 incident). Fixtures touched: NONE. The 12 new tests cover: fsync-precedes-rename asserted via monkeypatch (both writers); a failed fsync leaves the prior file intact; a corrupt ledger raises DailyStoreError naming --rebuild-ledger (not a raw pyarrow traceback); main() exits 2 with guidance; rebuild marks present dates and returns 404/error/absent dates to pending; rebuild skips a corrupt month; rebuild from a QUARANTINED month; rebuilt ledger reads back through the normal path; the --rebuild-ledger CLI needs no dates/network and reports a skipped corrupt month; a normal run with no dates asks for them or --rebuild-ledger.
decisions:
  - I1 Quarantine lives OUTSIDE the store's daily/ tree (data/daily_store/_quarantine/2026-07-25/, path-preserving + MANIFEST.txt), so the rebuild's `daily/*/bhavcopy_*.parquet` glob never re-scans it. Corrupt files are MOVED, never deleted (they are the only forensic record of the failure).
  - I2 The rebuild DERIVES the ledger from the row files alone and calls _write_ledger directly — it never reads the old ledger, so it works precisely when the ledger is the corrupt file. Each recovered date is file-present with source_format read from its stored rows, url reconstructed via url_for(day, fmt), http_status=200, row_count = that date's row count, reason=REBUILT_LEDGER_REASON (provenance), and attempted_at=None (the original fetch time is unknown; inventing one would be a lie the ledger later reads as fact).
  - I3 A monthly that will not open is SKIPPED and reported, not fatal — so --rebuild-ledger is safe even if a corrupt month was not quarantined first (its dates just return to pending). Lost confirmed-404/error knowledge is not fabricated; those dates become absent and re-resolve from fresh evidence on the next run (Q-3 safeguard 1 preserved).
  - I4 atomic_io fsyncs the file's DATA before os.replace in BOTH atomic_write_bytes and atomic_write_with (the un-fsynced path was the F6 hole every store/ledger write went through); the directory fsync that makes the rename itself durable is POSIX-only best-effort — Windows cannot open a directory fd and NTFS journals the rename, so it is a documented no-op there. The data-durability half, which is what prevents the incident's valid-name-over-garbage failure, runs on both platforms. This machine is Windows, so only the file fsync is active here; that is the half that mattered.
  - I5 --from/--to became optional at the parser so --rebuild-ledger can run range-free and offline; a normal run missing them prints a clear error naming --rebuild-ledger and exits 2. main() catches DailyStoreError and prints the guidance (exit 2) rather than letting a traceback reach the operator.
  - I6 The corrupt raw_bhav CSV (2009-03-13_archive.csv) was quarantined too, though it is the operator audit trail (--raw-dir), not the store the rebuild reads. It is a genuine corrupt power-loss artifact and a fixture-provenance hazard (REVIEW_2 F3, since the DERIVED fixtures were cut from that archive), so leaving it in place could poison a future fixture cut. Recorded as distinct from the two store files that drove recovery.
questions: none. Scope was fixed by the architect (recovery + F6 only) and CONTEXT.md answered everything needed (Q-3 ledger semantics govern what "pending again" means; F6's own text specified the fix). No new class-A hole hit; REVIEW_2 F7 remains open and is now partially mitigated (noted in its fix log) but not closed.

decisions:
  - B35 The Q-4 whitelist's RAISE takes priority over its precedence order. The ruling states both "EQ, else BE, else BZ" and "two whitelist series on one symbol-date → raise loudly"; since the raise fires first, the order never actually decides anything. It is kept as the documented reading of the ruling and as the report's ordering, not as live logic.
  - B36 `INSTRUMENT_SERIES` is one module constant and the ONLY series choice in `src/` (pinned by an `ast` walk). The non-instrument families are matched by FAMILY (`N*` debt, `P*` partly-paid, `BL`) rather than by an enumerated list, because NSE mints codes within a family freely — NTPC alone carried N4/N6/N7/NB/NC/ND across two days.
  - B37 `coverage_summary` counts weekend sessions from the LEDGER only (cheap, opens no month file); the series report is a separate, heavier query the backfill run calls once. A summary that had to read 300 month files would stop being callable after a Ctrl-C.
  - B38 The backfill implementation moved into the package; `scripts/backfill_daily.py` is a launcher whose module body executes nothing, with the `sys.path` fallback INSIDE a function that only `__main__` calls and that fires only when the package is not installed. Removing it entirely needs `pip install -e .`, and this session would not change the operator's environment while his 25-year backfill was running against that exact command (REVIEW_2 F12, half closed — stated as such in the fix log).
  - B39 `nse_http.cached_json` gained a `fetcher` hook rather than corp_actions growing its own cache. Three sources need three sessions, two decoders and their own pacing; only the POLICY (one live pull per day, opt-in, atomic write) must be shared, and now it provably is.
  - B40 Parse precedence puts DEMERGER first, ahead of every ratio kind. A demerger suppresses trading entirely (CONTEXT 3.2) while every other kind only scales a price, so a missed demerger is the costliest possible miss; "Scheme Of Arrangement / Bonus 1:2" resolves to demerger, and there is a test for exactly that.
  - B41 A dividend subject's cash components are SUMMED per ex-date ("Final Dividend Rs 6 Plus Special Dividend Rs 4" → 1000 paise). CONTEXT 4.2 classifies by SIZE against a reference price, not by the word "special", so what its threshold needs is the total cash per share leaving the company that day.
  - B42 The `informational` bucket requires BOTH a recognised meeting phrase AND no amount AND no ratio. Without the second half it would become a dustbin: "Annual General Meeting / Something Rs 5 Per Share" raises instead, because a subject carrying money this parser did not understand must be reported.
  - B43 A consolidation (reverse split) is treated as CONTEXT 4.2's own "face value A→B, k = B/A" row, but ONLY when the subject carries the From/To values a split needs. BSE's bare "Consolidation of Shares" therefore stays an exception rather than becoming a guess.
  - B44 The money regex accepts BSE's `Rs. - 5.5000` dash form. Measured: without it, every one of the 348 BSE dividend rows in the frozen windows became a parse exception — a parser bug wearing an exception report as camouflage.
  - B45 Corporate actions on a non-instrument SERIES are filtered out before parsing (opt-out via a flag). A coupon on a listed NCD is not an action on the equity we trade; this is the Q-4 ruling applied to a second data source, and it is what keeps the exception report readable — it removed every GS/IV/RR distribution row without a single special case.
  - B46 `adjust_pair` multiplies the whole factor chain at full `Decimal` precision and rounds ONCE, half-even, to integer paise (CONTEXT 7-E11). Rounding per event lets two chained events drift a paisa; half-even is unbiased, so a 25-year history of adjustments does not creep upward. An empty chain returns the price untouched with no rounding at all.
  - B47 Rights and dividend factors REQUIRE their prices and raise when absent; `build_factor_table` returns them as `pending` with the reason. A missing price must never become k = 1, because from the outside that is indistinguishable from "this event needs no adjustment" (Q-6, Q-7).
  - B48 The cross-source join matches on the KIND as well as the symbol-date. Found by the join itself: ROTO declared a bonus AND a dividend on 2023-07-07 and MAANALU a bonus AND a split on 2023-07-27, and a symbol-date-only join called both a disagreement when all three sources agreed on every event.
  - B49 BSE is joined by security NAME (CONTEXT 4.2: it publishes no ISIN), so a miss is reported as `not-found`, never as a disagreement. JMCPROJECT is the live case: NSE has its rights issue, BSE lists nothing under that name.
  - B50 Every network pull this session made ran at 1 request / 4s instead of the usual 1/2s, because the operator's 25-year backfill was running against the same infrastructure in another process. The combined rate stayed at the repo's usual pace.
  - B51 F8 READS NSE's calculator XLSX (stdlib `zipfile` + `ElementTree`, no new dependency) instead of only transcribing its numbers. A transcription cannot notice that it has drifted from its oracle; this also pins the relation CONTEXT 4.2 leaves implicit — NSE's bonus/split AF divides a strike (k = 1/AF) while its rights AF multiplies one (k = AF).
  - B52 `acumen.ca_report` is an entry point, not a library: it prints the two reports the card names, offline from the snapshots by default. It reads the F&O list from the cached pull if there is one and falls back to the frozen snapshot, so a reviewer with no cache gets the same output.

decisions:
  - B19 A date is only recorded as `confirmed-404` after BOTH published formats answer 404. CONTEXT 4.1 dates the UDiFF cutover only to the MONTH ("since Jul-2024"), so trusting a single boundary date would turn a run of real trading days into holidays if it is off by a few days — and under Q-3 that error is permanent and silent. Costs one extra request per non-trading day (~1.5h over the full 25-year run); buys immunity to a wrong boundary.
  - B20 Weekends are ATTEMPTED, not skipped by rule. The Q-3 ruling derives trading days from what NSE actually published, and NSE does hold weekend sessions; skipping Saturdays would replace the evidence with an assumption. It is also what produces the card's `confirmed-404` golden for Sunday 2026-07-19.
  - B21 The store is one parquet file per calendar MONTH (`daily/<YYYY>/bhavcopy_<YYYY>-<MM>.parquet`), not per date. ~21 dates x ~3,400 symbols is a healthy file; one file per date would leave chunks 3/4 opening 6,000+ files to read one symbol's history. Re-ingesting a date rewrites its month file with that date's rows replaced.
  - B22 The parquet schema is declared explicitly rather than inferred: an inferred schema changes shape when a month happens to have no nulls in a column, and a store whose dtypes drift between months is a store whose reads drift too.
  - B23 Reads come back as pandas nullable `Int64`, not the default conversion. A nullable int column converted the default way becomes float64, and a float cannot hold a turnover in paise exactly (TCS 2026-07-20: 497,162,363,920 paise).
  - B24 Prices are parsed to paise via `Decimal` on the CSV TEXT, and a value that is not a whole number of paise is REFUSED rather than rounded. `int(float("2189.20") * 100)` is 218919 — a paisa short of TCS's real 2026-07-15 close; rounding that is a guess about a price (CONTEXT 7-E11). *[Example corrected in place 2026-07-24 by the chunk-3 prep commit per REVIEW_2 Finding 4: the original entry cited `float("2251.10") * 100 == 225109.99999999997`, which is false — that product is exactly 225110.0. The decision is unchanged; only its illustration was wrong. The same false example survives verbatim in the body of commit 52791e0, which is history and is not rewritten.]*
  - B25 The column set kept is CONTEXT 4.1's OHLCV plus identity/liquidity fields (series, ISIN, instrument type, last, prev close, turnover, trades, source format). "Store everything" is applied to ROWS — every symbol and every series — not to the derivative-only UDiFF columns, which are empty in a cash-market file.
  - B26 `DailyStore.daily()` RAISES when a symbol-day is not unique instead of picking a row. Real data forces the issue and it is not rare (see Q-4). A silent choice would hand chunk 4 a block-deal or debenture candle on exactly the eventful days.
  - B27 `TradingCalendar` gained `trading_days` and `covered_days` (both optional, both `None` for a published calendar, chunk-1 behaviour bit-identical and pinned by a test) rather than a separate DerivedCalendar class. Everything downstream — `bias_pair`, `prev_trading_day`, the E2 filter — must behave identically whichever calendar it is handed, and one class is the only way to guarantee that.
  - B28 The derived calendar refuses to build unless EVERY date in its range is settled, instead of deriving around gaps and errors. This is Q-3 safeguard 1 made structural: an interrupted backfill cannot become a calendar of invented holidays. It also implements safeguard 3 for free — the current year can never be complete, so the published endpoint stays authoritative for today by construction.
  - B29 `scripts/` is not a package and nothing under `src/` imports it; its tests load it by path. It is an entry point, not a library.
  - B30 The backfill script gained `--raw-dir`, archiving each date's CSV exactly as NSE published it. `Download` carries `raw_csv` for this. It is the operator's audit trail and it is what the three DERIVED fixtures were cut from — a re-serialisation of parsed rows would not have been evidence (chunk-1 B16 applied to files).
  - B31 A shared retry loop now backs both `fetch_json` and the new `fetch_binary`, and `NseNotFoundError` makes 404 a TYPE rather than a string comparison. One loop so the CONTEXT 4.3 policy cannot drift between the JSON and download paths; a distinct type because the whole ledger depends on telling "absent" from "failed".
  - B32 `src/acumen/atomic_io.py` is one implementation of write-temp-then-replace, used by the day cache and by every store write, and it removes the temp file on any `BaseException` — `KeyboardInterrupt` is the case it exists for, since the operator is told the backfill is safe to interrupt.
  - B33 The offline guard now blocks at the SOCKET (`socket.socket.connect`/`connect_ex`, `socket.create_connection`) with the `requests` patches kept on top for their better message. `pytest-socket` was considered and declined: one more pinned dependency for ~10 lines, on a repo whose dependency list is deliberately short.
  - B34 The cross-source golden compares only what the two sources genuinely share, so no tolerance is invented anywhere: volume and O/H/L are asserted EXACT (25/25), while the daily close is NOT compared to the last 1-minute close (NSE's close is the last-30-minute weighted average — TCS 2026-07-20: 2251.10 vs an LTP of 2251.00, and the bhavcopy carries both). What is asserted for the close is the invariant that follows from its definition: it lies inside the 15:00-15:29 range. The volume-shortfall band is CONTEXT 4.5 gate 1's own [-0.1%, +5.0%], a spec number.

decisions:
  - B1 A third module, `nse_http.py`, holds the shared gentle-fetch + day-cache logic. The card names only universe.py and calendar.py, but both endpoints need identical access discipline (CONTEXT 4.1 "once/day max"); the alternatives were duplicating the retry/cache code or making calendar.py import universe.py.
  - B2 The network is opt-in: every fetch path defaults to `allow_network=False` and raises rather than reaching out. Enforced, not claimed — tests/conftest.py installs an autouse guard that FAILS any test making a real HTTP request, so a future default flipped to True cannot stay green while hammering NSE on every commit.
  - B3 `TradingCalendar` carries `covered_years` and RAISES `CalendarError` for any date outside them. The endpoint publishes one year at a time; treating an unloaded year as holiday-free would invent a dozen-odd phantom trading days a year and silently mis-pair every bias spanning one. This is the STOP rule made executable — see Q-3.
  - B4 `bias_pair` returns `BiasPair(current, previous)`, a NamedTuple that is still a plain tuple. CONTEXT 3.2's "current" is candle(D-1), the LATER of the two; named fields remove a silent bias inversion from chunk 4's reach.
  - B5 The E2 candle validator is defined by bar CONTAINMENT: a bar stamped t is in-session when `t >= 09:15` and `t + minutes <= 15:30`. Derived from E12 (open-stamped bars cover [t, t+minutes)) plus CONTEXT 4.5 gate 2 ("375 minutes 09:15-15:29"), so it needs no assumption and handles 1-min (09:15..15:29) and 15-min (09:15..15:15) bars with one rule.
  - B6 `SESSION_OPEN`/`SESSION_CLOSE` are module constants citing CONTEXT 3.1, deliberately NOT config keys: the exchange session is spec law, and a config key would let it move without an architect spec change.
  - B7 NSE's "26-Jan-2026" is parsed with an explicit month map, never `strptime("%d-%b-%Y")` — %b is LOCALE-dependent and would fail on a non-English machine. The parse is cross-checked in tests against the `weekDay` field NSE ships with each row (20/20 agree).
  - B8 Naive-IST is enforced loudly (CONTEXT 7-E8): a `datetime` passed where a `date` is expected raises rather than being truncated, and any tz-aware value raises. Directly addresses REVIEW_0 Finding 7 (the frozen PoC CSVs carry +05:30 stamps) — normalization is the ingest layer's job, not the calendar's.
  - B9 Universe symbols are stripped, upper-cased, de-duplicated and SORTED — the order is a property of the data, not of NSE's `serialNumber` field.
